In `_encode_image`:\\n   - Image pos embedding added\\n   - Temporal embedding added (for video, but X-VLA uses single frames)\\n   - Feature selection via `image_feature_source` (likely \\\"last_frame\\\")\\n   - Linear projection: `x @ self.image_projection` → `[B, N_tokens, projection_dim]`\\n   - LayerNorm applied\\n\\n3. In `forward_vlm`, the image features get merged with text embeddings and passed through Florence2 encoder to produce vlm_features\\n\\n4. In `SoftPromptedTransformer`, those vlm_features are projected to hidden_size, concatenated with action and auxiliary visual tokens, positional embeddings are added along with soft prompts, then everything flows through transformer blocks to decode the final action\\n\\nNow I'm thinking about where to prune tokens for efficiency. The cleanest point would be right after encoding the images, where I have pure vision tokens before they get mixed with text. I could prune based on L2 norm there. But once the Florence2 encoder processes everything, vision and text are already interleaved in vlm_features, so pruning at that stage wouldn't give me isolated vision tokens. Even at the SoftPromptedTransformer level, the vlm_features are already a blend. The real question is whether I should prune the pure vision tokens early or accept that I'm working with mixed representations downstream.\\n\\nI'm leaning toward implementing pruning right after `_encode_image` outputs those clean vision tokens with shape `[B, N_vis, projection_dim]`. I could either hook into that output or monkey-patch the forward_vlm method to inject the pruning logic. Monkey-patching seems cleaner for evaluation purposes. Now I need to figure out what N_vis actually is—for Florence-2 with different input resolutions, the DaViT backbone has specific patch strides that determine the final token count. There's a discrepancy between my calculation of 576 tokens and X-VLA's claim of 64 tokens—it could be a different input resolution, adaptive pooling, or specific DaViT configuration. return p.parse_args()\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# --- State preprocessing ---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef quat_to_axis_angle(quat: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\\\\\\\n    q = quat.astype(np.float64)\\\\\\\\n    w = np.clip(q[3], -1.0, 1.0)\\\\\\\\n    den = np.sqrt(max(1.0 - w * w, 0.0))\\\\\\\\n    if den > 1e-10:\\\\\\\\n        angle = 2.0 * np.arccos(w)\\\\\\\\n        axis = q[:3] / den\\\\\\\\n        return (axis * angle).astype(np.float32)\\\\\\\\n    return np.zeros(3, dtype=np.float32)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef extract_state(obs: dict) -> np.ndarray:\\\\\\\\n    rs = obs[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"robot_state\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]\\\\\\\\n    eef_pos = np.asarray(rs[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"eef\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"pos\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=np.float32)\\\\\\\\n    eef_quat = np.asarray(rs[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"eef\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"quat\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=np.float64)\\\\\\\\n    gripper_qpos = np.asarray(rs[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"gripper\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"qpos\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=np.float32)\\\\\\\\n    eef_aa = quat_to_axis_angle(eef_quat)\\\\\\\\n    return np.concatenate([eef_pos, eef_aa, gripper_qpos])\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef obs_to_batch(obs, task_text, tokenizer, camera_keys, device, norm_stats=None):\\\\\\\\n    batch = {}\\\\\\\\n    for cam_key in camera_keys:\\\\\\\\n        short_name = cam_key.split(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\".\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\")[-1]\\\\\\\\n        img = obs[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"pixels\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"][short_name]\\\\\\\\n        img_t = torch.from_numpy(np.asarray(img)).permute(2, 0, 1).float() / 255.0\\\\\\\\n        img_t = img_t.unsqueeze(0)\\\\\\\\n        img_t = torch.flip(img_t, dims=[2, 3])\\\\\\\\n        batch[cam_key] = img_t.to(device)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    state = extract_state(obs)\\\\\\\\n    state_t = torch.from_numpy(state).unsqueeze(0).to(device)\\\\\\\\n    if norm_stats is not None:\\\\\\\\n        state_t = normalize_state(state_t, norm_stats)\\\\\\\\n    batch[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"observation.state\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"] = state_t\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    text = task_text if task_text.endswith(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\") else task_text + \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n    encoded = tokenizer(\\\\\\\\n        [text], padding=\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"longest\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", max_length=48,\\\\\\\\n        truncation=True, return_tensors=\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"pt\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n    )\\\\\\\\n    batch[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"observation.language.tokens\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"] = encoded[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"input_ids\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to(device)\\\\\\\\n    batch[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"observation.language.attention_mask\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"] = encoded[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"attention_mask\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].bool().to(device)\\\\\\\\n    return batch\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# --- Model helpers ---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef find_connector(model):\\\\\\\\n    for name, module in model.named_modules():\\\\\\\\n        if name.endswith(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\".connector\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\") or \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"multi_modal_projector\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" in name:\\\\\\\\n            log.info(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Found connector: %s (%s)\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", name, type(module).__name__)\\\\\\\\n            return module\\\\\\\\n    return None\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef register_random_prune_hook(connector, k_ratio, method=\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"true_removal\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"):\\\\\\\\n    def hook_fn(module, input, output):\\\\\\\\n        tokens = output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output\\\\\\\\n        if tokens.dim() != 3:\\\\\\\\n            return output\\\\\\\\n        B, N, D = tokens.shape\\\\\\\\n        k = max(1, int(N * k_ratio))\\\\\\\\n        # Same random selection across batch for consistency\\\\\\\\n        scores = torch.rand(1, N, device=tokens.device).expand(B, -1)\\\\\\\\n        _, topk_idx = torch.topk(scores, k, dim=-1)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        if method == \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"true_removal\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\":\\\\\\\\n            kept = torch.zeros(N, dtype=torch.bool, device=tokens.device)\\\\\\\\n            kept[topk_idx[0]] = True\\\\\\\\n            masked = tokens[:, kept, :]\\\\\\\\n        else:\\\\\\\\n            mask = torch.zeros(B, N, device=tokens.device)\\\\\\\\n            mask.scatter_(1, topk_idx, 1.0)\\\\\\\\n            masked = tokens * mask.unsqueeze(-1)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        if isinstance(output, tuple):\\\\\\\\n            return (masked,) + output[1:]\\\\\\\\n        return masked\\\\\\\\n    return This means it uses `trust_remote_code=True` for loading.\\n\\n2. **Config details:**\\n   - `action_mode: \\\"ee6d\\\"` → 10D action internally, 7D output\\n   - `depth: 24`, `hidden_size: 1024`, `num_heads: 16`, `mlp_ratio: 4.0`\\n   - `num_actions: 30` (chunk_size/num_actions = 30, not 32 like lerobot default)\\n   - `num_domains: 30`, `len_soft_prompts: 32`\\n   - `use_proprio: true`, `use_hetero_proj: false`\\n   - `dim_time: 32`\\n\\n3. **Vision config:**\\n   - `dim_embed: [256, 512, 1024, 2048]` (DaViT stages)\\n   - `patch_stride: [4, 2, 2, 2]` → total downsample = 32x\\n   - `image_feature_source: [\\\"spatial_avg_pool\\\", \\\"temporal_avg_pool\\\"]`\\n   - `projection_dim: 1024`\\n\\n4. **Key: `image_feature_source: [\\\"spatial_avg_pool\\\", \\\"temporal_avg_pool\\\"]`**\\n\\nThe feature extraction uses both spatial and temporal pooling operations. Spatial pooling averages across the spatial dimensions to get per-frame embeddings, while temporal pooling averages across frames to get per-location embeddings.\\n\\nThese two token streams get concatenated together, so for a 256x256 input with 32x downsampling, I end up with 1 token from spatial pooling plus 64 tokens from temporal pooling, giving 65 total vision tokens.\\n\\nAfter projection through the image projection layer and layer norm, these become `[B, 65, 1024]` embeddings.\\n\\nLooking more closely at the `_encode_image` function, when the input is a single image (4D tensor), the vision tower's `forward_features_unpool` produces `[B, 64, 2048]` for 256x256 input.\\n\\nReshaping and continuing... I'm tracking how the spatial and temporal pooling operations reshape the tensor, then concatenate them to create 65 vision tokens total, which gets projected down to 1024 dimensions before pruning to keep only 19 tokens when k=0.3.\\n\\nThe spatial average pool token is crucial since it summarizes all spatial information, but I think the L2-norm pruning will naturally preserve it anyway since it likely has high magnitude. I'll keep the approach simple and prune all 65 tokens uniformly by L2 norm rather than special-casing the first token.\\n\\nNow I need to account for the fact that this is the original X-VLA format, not the lerobot format, so the model loading will be different. I'm realizing there's a version mismatch—the lerobot installation has its own `modeling_xvla.py` that differs from the original X-VLA repo on HuggingFace, and since the model weights were saved with the original code, I need to figure out which implementation is actually compatible with those weights. Let me check the original X-VLA repository to understand what I'm working with.
